========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, um… I guess what brought me in is I’ve been kind of going way beyond my normal lane at work. I’m usually just one of the crew guys, you know, mowing, planting, hauling stuff around, whatever needs doing.

But lately I started trying to run like three different bids at once, calling people at the city, trying to line up plant suppliers, messaging everybody I know about this park idea I had. And it’s like I couldn’t shut it off. I’d start with one thing and then think, oh, I should call this guy, and then there’s this other nursery out by where my cousin lives—anyway, it just got to be a lot, and people were looking at me like I was doing too much.

Yeah… I mean, happy isn’t exactly the word, but kind of. I felt really fired up, like I was bigger than myself or something, like I could make all this stuff happen if people would just get out of the way.

And it’s like I couldn’t shut it off. I’d start with one thing and then think, oh, I should call this guy, and then there’s this other nursery out by where my cousin lives—anyway, it just got to be a lot, and people were looking at me like I was doing too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or excited lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, happy isn’t exactly the word, but kind of. Before I kind of crashed, I was really fired up—like, I don’t know, almost bigger than myself, like I had this whole plan and everybody should just see it.\n\nBut yeah, if one of the guys questioned me or said I should slow down, I’d snap. Like, way harder than made sense. Even little stuff felt like they were getting in the way. I remember one coworker just asking if we had approval for something, and I went off on him like he’d insulted me or something. Then later I’m thinking, why did I do that? But at the time it felt totally justified.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty at any point recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… sad or empty? I mean, maybe tired of all of it, but I’m not sure I’d call it empty. Like, sometimes after work I just sit there and the TV’s on but I’m not really watching, you know?\n\nBut then again, I do that anyway sometimes. My dad used to do that after long days too, just sit in his chair with the news on and not say anything, so I don’t know if that means anything. I guess I haven’t really been thinking of it as “sad,” exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're more important or capable than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. I mean, it sounds stupid saying it now, but I honestly thought I was about to be the guy every town around here called for sustainable landscaping. Like if anybody needed it done right, they’d come to me.\n\nAnd the funny thing is, I’m still just trying to keep my small jobs paid up, you know? Like I’ve got regular work, bills, equipment stuff, all that. My cousin was saying the other day how expensive mulch has gotten—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, in my head, it felt like I was way past where I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being fired up and capable something that you experience all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes. It’s not like I’m like that all the time.\n\nThat stretch started when I was kind of remaking myself for that park pitch—like I was getting all into it, thinking about how I’d present myself, what I’d say, even stuff like how the truck looked, which is dumb maybe, but it felt important. I don’t really know. I mean, my work kind of has seasons, so it’s hard to separate it out. Like spring rolls around and suddenly everyone wants mulch, lawns cleaned up, beds edged, all that stuff, and you’re just moving more anyway. I guess I haven’t tracked it like that. Half the time I only realize afterward that I was running on that fired-up feeling, and then I’m like, “Was that because of work, or the weather, or just me?” I’m not sure.

Like way harder than it called for. I’d hear myself afterward and be like, why did I come at him like that? Like, worse than the usual heaviness. And it’s weird because work stuff keeps going, grass keeps growing, people still want things done, my truck still needs gas, but I’m just… under it. It’s been a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low periods, like the one you're in now, happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, winter can feel worse just because everything’s gray and work slows down, and then I start thinking too much, you know? But I don’t know if that’s a pattern or just… life being life.\n\nI’ve never really marked it down like, “Oh, every January I get low,” or anything. It’s more like I notice I’m already in it, and then afterward I’m trying to remember when it started, and it all gets kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the times when you feel fired up and capable are related to a specific season or time of year?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my work kind of has seasons, so it’s hard to separate it out. Like spring rolls around and suddenly everyone wants mulch, lawns cleaned up, beds edged, all that stuff, and you’re just moving more anyway. So maybe I feel more capable then because there’s more to do? But I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some specific season where it always happens.\n\nI guess I haven’t tracked it like that. Half the time I only realize afterward that I was running on that fired-up feeling, and then I’m like, “Was that because of work, or the weather, or just me?” I’m not sure.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 29 / landscaping_or_groundskeeping_worker / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressive Episode Duration\nmanifestation: \"The depression has been hanging over me for about a year, and this latest crash is the worst it’s gotten.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say always, but… yeah, it seems like I end up down after. Or maybe I’m already kind of down and then that fired-up part just makes it look different for a while.

The thing is, this depressed feeling has been hanging around for about a year now. Like it’s just been there in the background while I’m mowing lawns or fixing equipment or whatever, and then this latest crash after feeling capable and all that… that’s the worst it’s gotten. So I guess after those times, yeah, it can really drop off hard.

Yeah, I mean… I think I already kind of went into this, but it does feel like I end up depressed after those fired-up stretches. This low feeling’s been hanging over me for about a year, honestly.

It’s hard to tell where one thing ends and the other starts, because life and work just keep going, you know? But yeah, the depression’s been there a long time, and lately it’s gotten really bad.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess what brought me in is I’ve been kind of going way beyond my normal lane at work. I’m usually just one of the crew guys, you know, mowing, planting, hauling stuff around, whatever needs doing.\n\nBut lately I started trying to run like three different bids at once, calling people at the city, trying to line up plant suppliers, messaging everybody I know about this park idea I had. And it’s like I couldn’t shut it off. I’d start with one thing and then think, oh, I should call this guy, and then there’s this other nursery out by where my cousin lives—anyway, it just got to be a lot, and people were looking at me like I was doing too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or excited lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, happy isn’t exactly the word, but kind of. I felt really fired up, like I was bigger than myself or something, like I could make all this stuff happen if people would just get out of the way.\n\nAnd then if one of the guys at work was like, “Maybe slow down,” or questioned what I was doing, I’d just snap. Like way harder than it called for. I’d hear myself afterward and be like, why did I come at him like that? But in the moment it felt like they were messing up this whole thing I had going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable or snapping at people consistently over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Before I kind of crashed, I was really fired up—like, I don’t know, almost bigger than myself, like I had this whole plan and everybody should just see it.\n\nBut yeah, if one of the guys questioned me or said I should slow down, I’d snap. Like, way harder than made sense. Even little stuff felt like they were getting in the way. I remember one coworker just asking if we had approval for something, and I went off on him like he’d insulted me or something. Then later I’m thinking, why did I do that? But at the time it felt totally justified.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty at any point recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… sad or empty? I mean, maybe tired of all of it, but I’m not sure I’d call it empty. Like, sometimes after work I just sit there and the TV’s on but I’m not really watching, you know?\n\nBut then again, I do that anyway sometimes. My dad used to do that after long days too, just sit in his chair with the news on and not say anything, so I don’t know if that means anything. I guess I haven’t really been thinking of it as “sad,” exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're more important or capable than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. I mean, it sounds stupid saying it now, but I honestly thought I was about to be the guy every town around here called for sustainable landscaping. Like if anybody needed it done right, they’d come to me.\n\nAnd the funny thing is, I’m still just trying to keep my small jobs paid up, you know? Like I’ve got regular work, bills, equipment stuff, all that. My cousin was saying the other day how expensive mulch has gotten—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, in my head, it felt like I was way past where I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being fired up and capable something that you experience all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes. It’s not like I’m like that all the time.\n\nThat stretch started when I was kind of remaking myself for that park pitch—like I was getting all into it, thinking about how I’d present myself, what I’d say, even stuff like how the truck looked, which is dumb maybe, but it felt important. And then it just kept going for… I don’t know, about two weeks, maybe. Then I kind of crashed after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these periods of feeling fired up and capable happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being the same time every year. Like, I’m trying to think—spring gets busy for landscaping, obviously, so everybody’s kind of running around then, and fall cleanup has its own thing too. \n\nBut whether I get that… fired-up feeling on a calendar schedule? I couldn’t really tell you. I haven’t kept track like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of feeling really down or depressed after one of these episodes where you felt fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… after that kind of high point, I do come down, but honestly it’s not even like it just started then. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for… I don’t know, about a year now.\n\nThis last crash after feeling all fired up, though—that’s been the worst it’s gotten. Like, worse than the usual heaviness. And it’s weird because work stuff keeps going, grass keeps growing, people still want things done, my truck still needs gas, but I’m just… under it. It’s been a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low periods, like the one you're in now, happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, winter can feel worse just because everything’s gray and work slows down, and then I start thinking too much, you know? But I don’t know if that’s a pattern or just… life being life.\n\nI’ve never really marked it down like, “Oh, every January I get low,” or anything. It’s more like I notice I’m already in it, and then afterward I’m trying to remember when it started, and it all gets kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the times when you feel fired up and capable are related to a specific season or time of year?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my work kind of has seasons, so it’s hard to separate it out. Like spring rolls around and suddenly everyone wants mulch, lawns cleaned up, beds edged, all that stuff, and you’re just moving more anyway. So maybe I feel more capable then because there’s more to do? But I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some specific season where it always happens.\n\nI guess I haven’t tracked it like that. Half the time I only realize afterward that I was running on that fired-up feeling, and then I’m like, “Was that because of work, or the weather, or just me?” I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, but… yeah, it seems like I end up down after. Or maybe I’m already kind of down and then that fired-up part just makes it look different for a while.\n\nThe thing is, this depressed feeling has been hanging around for about a year now. Like it’s just been there in the background while I’m mowing lawns or fixing equipment or whatever, and then this latest crash after feeling capable and all that… that’s the worst it’s gotten. So I guess after those times, yeah, it can really drop off hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think I already kind of went into this, but it does feel like I end up depressed after those fired-up stretches. It varies, I think… or at least I don’t have a clean pattern for it. This one hasn’t felt like a normal “couple days and then I’m back” kind of thing. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for about a year now, like even when work is busy and I’m out doing jobs, it’s still there.

I’m usually just one of the crew guys, you know, mowing, planting, hauling stuff around, whatever needs doing.\n\nBut lately I started trying to run like three different bids at once, calling people at the city, trying to line up plant suppliers, messaging everybody I know about this park idea I had. And it’s like I couldn’t shut it off. I’d start with one thing and then think, oh, I should call this guy, and then there’s this other nursery out by where my cousin lives—anyway, it just got to be a lot, and people were looking at me like I was doing too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or excited lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, happy isn’t exactly the word, but kind of. I felt really fired up, like I was bigger than myself or something, like I could make all this stuff happen if people would just get out of the way.\n\nAnd then if one of the guys at work was like, “Maybe slow down,” or questioned what I was doing, I’d just snap. Like way harder than it called for. I’d hear myself afterward and be like, why did I come at him like that? But in the moment it felt like they were messing up this whole thing I had going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable or snapping at people consistently over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Before I kind of crashed, I was really fired up—like, I don’t know, almost bigger than myself, like I had this whole plan and everybody should just see it.\n\nBut yeah, if one of the guys questioned me or said I should slow down, I’d snap. Like, way harder than made sense. Even little stuff felt like they were getting in the way. I remember one coworker just asking if we had approval for something, and I went off on him like he’d insulted me or something. Then later I’m thinking, why did I do that? But at the time it felt totally justified.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty at any point recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… sad or empty? I mean, maybe tired of all of it, but I’m not sure I’d call it empty. Like, sometimes after work I just sit there and the TV’s on but I’m not really watching, you know?\n\nBut then again, I do that anyway sometimes. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess what brought me in is I’ve been kind of going way beyond my normal lane at work. I’m usually just one of the crew guys, you know, mowing, planting, hauling stuff around, whatever needs doing.\n\nBut lately I started trying to run like three different bids at once, calling people at the city, trying to line up plant suppliers, messaging everybody I know about this park idea I had. And it’s like I couldn’t shut it off. I’d start with one thing and then think, oh, I should call this guy, and then there’s this other nursery out by where my cousin lives—anyway, it just got to be a lot, and people were looking at me like I was doing too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or excited lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, happy isn’t exactly the word, but kind of. I felt really fired up, like I was bigger than myself or something, like I could make all this stuff happen if people would just get out of the way.\n\nAnd then if one of the guys at work was like, “Maybe slow down,” or questioned what I was doing, I’d just snap. Like way harder than it called for. I’d hear myself afterward and be like, why did I come at him like that? But in the moment it felt like they were messing up this whole thing I had going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable or snapping at people consistently over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Before I kind of crashed, I was really fired up—like, I don’t know, almost bigger than myself, like I had this whole plan and everybody should just see it.\n\nBut yeah, if one of the guys questioned me or said I should slow down, I’d snap. Like, way harder than made sense. Even little stuff felt like they were getting in the way. I remember one coworker just asking if we had approval for something, and I went off on him like he’d insulted me or something. Then later I’m thinking, why did I do that? But at the time it felt totally justified.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty at any point recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… sad or empty? I mean, maybe tired of all of it, but I’m not sure I’d call it empty. Like, sometimes after work I just sit there and the TV’s on but I’m not really watching, you know?\n\nBut then again, I do that anyway sometimes. My dad used to do that after long days too, just sit in his chair with the news on and not say anything, so I don’t know if that means anything. I guess I haven’t really been thinking of it as “sad,” exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're more important or capable than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. I mean, it sounds stupid saying it now, but I honestly thought I was about to be the guy every town around here called for sustainable landscaping. Like if anybody needed it done right, they’d come to me.\n\nAnd the funny thing is, I’m still just trying to keep my small jobs paid up, you know? Like I’ve got regular work, bills, equipment stuff, all that. My cousin was saying the other day how expensive mulch has gotten—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, in my head, it felt like I was way past where I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being fired up and capable something that you experience all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes. It’s not like I’m like that all the time.\n\nThat stretch started when I was kind of remaking myself for that park pitch—like I was getting all into it, thinking about how I’d present myself, what I’d say, even stuff like how the truck looked, which is dumb maybe, but it felt important. And then it just kept going for… I don’t know, about two weeks, maybe. Then I kind of crashed after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these periods of feeling fired up and capable happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being the same time every year. Like, I’m trying to think—spring gets busy for landscaping, obviously, so everybody’s kind of running around then, and fall cleanup has its own thing too. \n\nBut whether I get that… fired-up feeling on a calendar schedule? I couldn’t really tell you. I haven’t kept track like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of feeling really down or depressed after one of these episodes where you felt fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… after that kind of high point, I do come down, but honestly it’s not even like it just started then. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for… I don’t know, about a year now.\n\nThis last crash after feeling all fired up, though—that’s been the worst it’s gotten. Like, worse than the usual heaviness. And it’s weird because work stuff keeps going, grass keeps growing, people still want things done, my truck still needs gas, but I’m just… under it. It’s been a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low periods, like the one you're in now, happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, winter can feel worse just because everything’s gray and work slows down, and then I start thinking too much, you know? But I don’t know if that’s a pattern or just… life being life.\n\nI’ve never really marked it down like, “Oh, every January I get low,” or anything. It’s more like I notice I’m already in it, and then afterward I’m trying to remember when it started, and it all gets kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the times when you feel fired up and capable are related to a specific season or time of year?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my work kind of has seasons, so it’s hard to separate it out. Like spring rolls around and suddenly everyone wants mulch, lawns cleaned up, beds edged, all that stuff, and you’re just moving more anyway. So maybe I feel more capable then because there’s more to do? But I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some specific season where it always happens.\n\nI guess I haven’t tracked it like that. Half the time I only realize afterward that I was running on that fired-up feeling, and then I’m like, “Was that because of work, or the weather, or just me?” I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, but… yeah, it seems like I end up down after. Or maybe I’m already kind of down and then that fired-up part just makes it look different for a while.\n\nThe thing is, this depressed feeling has been hanging around for about a year now. Like it’s just been there in the background while I’m mowing lawns or fixing equipment or whatever, and then this latest crash after feeling capable and all that… that’s the worst it’s gotten. So I guess after those times, yeah, it can really drop off hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think I already kind of went into this, but it does feel like I end up depressed after those fired-up stretches. Like way harder than it called for. I’d hear myself afterward and be like, why did I come at him like that? But in the moment it felt like they were messing up this whole thing I had going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable or snapping at people consistently over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Like, way harder than made sense. Even little stuff felt like they were getting in the way. I mean, maybe tired of all of it, but I’m not sure I’d call it empty. Like, sometimes after work I just sit there and the TV’s on but I’m not really watching, you know?\n\nBut then again, I do that anyway sometimes. My dad used to do that after long days too, just sit in his chair with the news on and not say anything, so I don’t know if that means anything. I guess I haven’t really been thinking of it as “sad,” exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're more important or capable than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. I mean, it sounds stupid saying it now, but I honestly thought I was about to be the guy every town around here called for sustainable landscaping. Like if anybody needed it done right, they’d come to me.\n\nAnd the funny thing is, I’m still just trying to keep my small jobs paid up, you know? Like I’ve got regular work, bills, equipment stuff, all that. My cousin was saying the other day how expensive mulch has gotten—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, in my head, it felt like I was way past where I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being fired up and capable something that you experience all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes. It’s not like I’m like that all the time.\n\nThat stretch started when I was kind of remaking myself for that park pitch—like I was getting all into it, thinking about how I’d present myself, what I’d say, even stuff like how the truck looked, which is dumb maybe, but it felt important. And then it just kept going for… I don’t know, about two weeks, maybe. Then I kind of crashed after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these periods of feeling fired up and capable happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being the same time every year. Like, I’m trying to think—spring gets busy for landscaping, obviously, so everybody’s kind of running around then, and fall cleanup has its own thing too. \n\nBut whether I get that… fired-up feeling on a calendar schedule? I couldn’t really tell you. I haven’t kept track like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of feeling really down or depressed after one of these episodes where you felt fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… after that kind of high point, I do come down, but honestly it’s not even like it just started then. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for… I don’t know, about a year now.\n\nThis last crash after feeling all fired up, though—that’s been the worst it’s gotten. Like, worse than the usual heaviness. And it’s weird because work stuff keeps going, grass keeps growing, people still want things done, my truck still needs gas, but I’m just… under it. It’s been a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low periods, like the one you're in now, happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, winter can feel worse just because everything’s gray and work slows down, and then I start thinking too much, you know? But I don’t know if that’s a pattern or just… life being life.\n\nI’ve never really marked it down like, “Oh, every January I get low,” or anything. It’s more like I notice I’m already in it, and then afterward I’m trying to remember when it started, and it all gets kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the times when you feel fired up and capable are related to a specific season or time of year?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my work kind of has seasons, so it’s hard to separate it out. Like spring rolls around and suddenly everyone wants mulch, lawns cleaned up, beds edged, all that stuff, and you’re just moving more anyway. So maybe I feel more capable then because there’s more to do? But I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some specific season where it always happens.\n\nI guess I haven’t tracked it like that. Half the time I only realize afterward that I was running on that fired-up feeling, and then I’m like, “Was that because of work, or the weather, or just me?” I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, but… yeah, it seems like I end up down after. Or maybe I’m already kind of down and then that fired-up part just makes it look different for a while.\n\nThe thing is, this depressed feeling has been hanging around for about a year now. The point is, in my head, it felt like I was way past where I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being fired up and capable something that you experience all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes. It’s not like I’m like that all the time.\n\nThat stretch started when I was kind of remaking myself for that park pitch—like I was getting all into it, thinking about how I’d present myself, what I’d say, even stuff like how the truck looked, which is dumb maybe, but it felt important. And then it just kept going for… I don’t know, about two weeks, maybe. Then I kind of crashed after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these periods of feeling fired up and capable happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being the same time every year. Like, I’m trying to think—spring gets busy for landscaping, obviously, so everybody’s kind of running around then, and fall cleanup has its own thing too. \n\nBut whether I get that… fired-up feeling on a calendar schedule? I couldn’t really tell you. I haven’t kept track like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of feeling really down or depressed after one of these episodes where you felt fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… after that kind of high point, I do come down, but honestly it’s not even like it just started then. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for… I don’t know, about a year now.\n\nThis last crash after feeling all fired up, though—that’s been the worst it’s gotten. Like, worse than the usual heaviness. And it’s weird because work stuff keeps going, grass keeps growing, people still want things done, my truck still needs gas, but I’m just… under it. It’s been a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low periods, like the one you're in now, happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, winter can feel worse just because everything’s gray and work slows down, and then I start thinking too much, you know? But I don’t know if that’s a pattern or just… life being life.\n\nI’ve never really marked it down like, “Oh, every January I get low,” or anything. It’s more like I notice I’m already in it, and then afterward I’m trying to remember when it started, and it all gets kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the times when you feel fired up and capable are related to a specific season or time of year?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my work kind of has seasons, so it’s hard to separate it out. Like spring rolls around and suddenly everyone wants mulch, lawns cleaned up, beds edged, all that stuff, and you’re just moving more anyway. So maybe I feel more capable then because there’s more to do? But I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some specific season where it always happens.\n\nI guess I haven’t tracked it like that. Half the time I only realize afterward that I was running on that fired-up feeling, and then I’m like, “Was that because of work, or the weather, or just me?” I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, but… yeah, it seems like I end up down after. Or maybe I’m already kind of down and then that fired-up part just makes it look different for a while.\n\nThe thing is, this depressed feeling has been hanging around for about a year now. Like it’s just been there in the background while I’m mowing lawns or fixing equipment or whatever, and then this latest crash after feeling capable and all that… that’s the worst it’s gotten. So I guess after those times, yeah, it can really drop off hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think I already kind of went into this, but it does feel like I end up depressed after those fired-up stretches. It’s hard to tell where one thing ends and the other starts, because life and work just keep going, you know? The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for about a year now, like even when work is busy and I’m out doing jobs, it’s still there.\n\nAnd this latest crash after feeling all fired up has been the worst it’s gotten. I couldn’t really tell you if the other times were the same length, because I don’t write it down or anything, and days kind of blur together when you’re just trying to keep up with work. But yeah… this time it’s been going on a long time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that when you're not in one of these fired-up periods, you're generally a more subdued or less confident person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably. I felt really fired up, like I was bigger than myself or something, like I could make all this stuff happen if people would just get out of the way.\n\nAnd then if one of the guys at work was like, “Maybe slow down,” or questioned what I was doing, I’d just snap. Like way harder than it called for. I’d hear myself afterward and be like, why did I come at him like that? But in the moment it felt like they were messing up this whole thing I had going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable or snapping at people consistently over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Before I kind of crashed, I was really fired up—like, I don’t know, almost bigger than myself, like I had this whole plan and everybody should just see it.\n\nBut yeah, if one of the guys questioned me or said I should slow down, I’d snap. Like, way harder than made sense. Even little stuff felt like they were getting in the way. I remember one coworker just asking if we had approval for something, and I went off on him like he’d insulted me or something. Then later I’m thinking, why did I do that? But at the time it felt totally justified.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty at any point recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… sad or empty? I mean, maybe tired of all of it, but I’m not sure I’d call it empty. Like, sometimes after work I just sit there and the TV’s on but I’m not really watching, you know?\n\nBut then again, I do that anyway sometimes. My dad used to do that after long days too, just sit in his chair with the news on and not say anything, so I don’t know if that means anything. I guess I haven’t really been thinking of it as “sad,” exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're more important or capable than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. I mean, it sounds stupid saying it now, but I honestly thought I was about to be the guy every town around here called for sustainable landscaping. Like if anybody needed it done right, they’d come to me.\n\nAnd the funny thing is, I’m still just trying to keep my small jobs paid up, you know? Like I’ve got regular work, bills, equipment stuff, all that. My cousin was saying the other day how expensive mulch has gotten—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, in my head, it felt like I was way past where I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being fired up and capable something that you experience all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes. It’s not like I’m like that all the time.\n\nThat stretch started when I was kind of remaking myself for that park pitch—like I was getting all into it, thinking about how I’d present myself, what I’d say, even stuff like how the truck looked, which is dumb maybe, but it felt important. And then it just kept going for… I don’t know, about two weeks, maybe. Then I kind of crashed after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these periods of feeling fired up and capable happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being the same time every year. Like, I’m trying to think—spring gets busy for landscaping, obviously, so everybody’s kind of running around then, and fall cleanup has its own thing too. \n\nBut whether I get that… fired-up feeling on a calendar schedule? I couldn’t really tell you. I haven’t kept track like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of feeling really down or depressed after one of these episodes where you felt fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… after that kind of high point, I do come down, but honestly it’s not even like it just started then. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for… I don’t know, about a year now.\n\nThis last crash after feeling all fired up, though—that’s been the worst it’s gotten. Like, worse than the usual heaviness. And it’s weird because work stuff keeps going, grass keeps growing, people still want things done, my truck still needs gas, but I’m just… under it. It’s been a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low periods, like the one you're in now, happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, winter can feel worse just because everything’s gray and work slows down, and then I start thinking too much, you know? But I don’t know if that’s a pattern or just… life being life.\n\nI’ve never really marked it down like, “Oh, every January I get low,” or anything. It’s more like I notice I’m already in it, and then afterward I’m trying to remember when it started, and it all gets kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the times when you feel fired up and capable are related to a specific season or time of year?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my work kind of has seasons, so it’s hard to separate it out. Like spring rolls around and suddenly everyone wants mulch, lawns cleaned up, beds edged, all that stuff, and you’re just moving more anyway. So maybe I feel more capable then because there’s more to do? But I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some specific season where it always happens.\n\nI guess I haven’t tracked it like that. Half the time I only realize afterward that I was running on that fired-up feeling, and then I’m like, “Was that because of work, or the weather, or just me?” I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, but… yeah, it seems like I end up down after. Or maybe I’m already kind of down and then that fired-up part just makes it look different for a while.\n\nThe thing is, this depressed feeling has been hanging around for about a year now. Like it’s just been there in the background while I’m mowing lawns or fixing equipment or whatever, and then this latest crash after feeling capable and all that… that’s the worst it’s gotten. So I guess after those times, yeah, it can really drop off hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think I already kind of went into this, but it does feel like I end up depressed after those fired-up stretches. This low feeling’s been hanging over me for about a year, honestly.\n\nAnd this last time, after I had that “I can handle everything” kind of stretch, the crash has been the worst it’s gotten. It’s hard to tell where one thing ends and the other starts, because life and work just keep going, you know? But yeah, the depression’s been there a long time, and lately it’s gotten really bad.\n\nDoctor: Is the depressed feeling that follows your fired-up episodes usually the same length of time, or does it vary?\n\nPatient: It varies, I think… or at least I don’t have a clean pattern for it. This one hasn’t felt like a normal “couple days and then I’m back” kind of thing. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for about a year now, like even when work is busy and I’m out doing jobs, it’s still there.\n\nAnd this latest crash after feeling all fired up has been the worst it’s gotten. I couldn’t really tell you if the other times were the same length, because I don’t write it down or anything, and days kind of blur together when you’re just trying to keep up with work. But yeah… this time it’s been going on a long time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that when you're not in one of these fired-up periods, you're generally a more subdued or less confident person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably. When I’m not in that mode, I’m more like just trying to get through the day and keep the jobs paid up, you know?\n\nBut when I’m in it, it’s different. I really believed I was about to become, like, the guy every town around here would call for sustainable landscaping. Like I had this whole picture in my head of being known for it, everybody needing me for that kind of work… even though, realistically, I’m still just doing small jobs and trying to keep everything from falling behind.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I feel like I already kind of said it, but yeah, it usually seems like I come down afterward. This time especially, it’s not like I just felt lousy for a weekend or something.\n\nIt’s been… hanging over me for about a year now, honestly. And after the most recent time where I felt really fired up and like I could take on everything, the crash after that has been the worst it’s gotten. I still go to work and do what I have to do, mostly, but it’s just been sitting there over me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not in one of these periods where you feel fired up and capable, do you feel completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I guess I keep thinking “normal” should mean I’m just back to myself, going to work, doing the yards, dealing with customers, all that. And I do some of that, but it’s like there’s this weight still there.\n\nHonestly, the depression’s been hanging over me for about a year now. It’s not like I come down and then I’m fine again after a couple days. This latest crash has been the worst it’s gotten… like even when I’m technically functioning, I don’t feel back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can recall specific times when you shifted from feeling normal to feeling fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I can think of times. It’s not always like a light switch, but before I crashed I remember feeling really fired up, like… almost bigger than myself, I guess. Like I could handle way more than I normally would.\n\nAnd it would show up at work too. If one of the guys questioned my plan or told me I should slow down, I’d snap at him way harder than made sense. Like, over something small, and then later I’d think, why did I come at him like that? But in the moment it felt like they were getting in the way, you know?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

This time especially, it’s not like I just felt lousy for a weekend or something.\n\nIt’s been… hanging over me for about a year now, honestly. I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t really keep track of it that way. My sleep’s kind of tied up with work sometimes, like if we’ve got an early job or weather coming in, I’ll be up thinking about routes or equipment or whatever… but I don’t know if that’s the same as needing less sleep.

So… I guess I can’t really say for sure.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF31.0, F31.1, F34.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess what brought me in is I’ve been kind of going way beyond my normal lane at work. I’m usually just one of the crew guys, you know, mowing, planting, hauling stuff around, whatever needs doing.\n\nBut lately I started trying to run like three different bids at once, calling people at the city, trying to line up plant suppliers, messaging everybody I know about this park idea I had. And it’s like I couldn’t shut it off. I’d start with one thing and then think, oh, I should call this guy, and then there’s this other nursery out by where my cousin lives—anyway, it just got to be a lot, and people were looking at me like I was doing too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or excited lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, happy isn’t exactly the word, but kind of. I felt really fired up, like I was bigger than myself or something, like I could make all this stuff happen if people would just get out of the way.\n\nAnd then if one of the guys at work was like, “Maybe slow down,” or questioned what I was doing, I’d just snap. Like way harder than it called for. I’d hear myself afterward and be like, why did I come at him like that? But in the moment it felt like they were messing up this whole thing I had going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable or snapping at people consistently over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Before I kind of crashed, I was really fired up—like, I don’t know, almost bigger than myself, like I had this whole plan and everybody should just see it.\n\nBut yeah, if one of the guys questioned me or said I should slow down, I’d snap. Like, way harder than made sense. Even little stuff felt like they were getting in the way. I remember one coworker just asking if we had approval for something, and I went off on him like he’d insulted me or something. Then later I’m thinking, why did I do that? But at the time it felt totally justified.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty at any point recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… sad or empty? I mean, maybe tired of all of it, but I’m not sure I’d call it empty. Like, sometimes after work I just sit there and the TV’s on but I’m not really watching, you know?\n\nBut then again, I do that anyway sometimes. My dad used to do that after long days too, just sit in his chair with the news on and not say anything, so I don’t know if that means anything. I guess I haven’t really been thinking of it as “sad,” exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're more important or capable than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. I mean, it sounds stupid saying it now, but I honestly thought I was about to be the guy every town around here called for sustainable landscaping. Like if anybody needed it done right, they’d come to me.\n\nAnd the funny thing is, I’m still just trying to keep my small jobs paid up, you know? Like I’ve got regular work, bills, equipment stuff, all that. My cousin was saying the other day how expensive mulch has gotten—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, in my head, it felt like I was way past where I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being fired up and capable something that you experience all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes. It’s not like I’m like that all the time.\n\nThat stretch started when I was kind of remaking myself for that park pitch—like I was getting all into it, thinking about how I’d present myself, what I’d say, even stuff like how the truck looked, which is dumb maybe, but it felt important. And then it just kept going for… I don’t know, about two weeks, maybe. Then I kind of crashed after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these periods of feeling fired up and capable happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being the same time every year. Like, I’m trying to think—spring gets busy for landscaping, obviously, so everybody’s kind of running around then, and fall cleanup has its own thing too. \n\nBut whether I get that… fired-up feeling on a calendar schedule? I couldn’t really tell you. I haven’t kept track like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of feeling really down or depressed after one of these episodes where you felt fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… after that kind of high point, I do come down, but honestly it’s not even like it just started then. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for… I don’t know, about a year now.\n\nThis last crash after feeling all fired up, though—that’s been the worst it’s gotten. Like, worse than the usual heaviness. And it’s weird because work stuff keeps going, grass keeps growing, people still want things done, my truck still needs gas, but I’m just… under it. It’s been a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low periods, like the one you're in now, happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, winter can feel worse just because everything’s gray and work slows down, and then I start thinking too much, you know? But I don’t know if that’s a pattern or just… life being life.\n\nI’ve never really marked it down like, “Oh, every January I get low,” or anything. It’s more like I notice I’m already in it, and then afterward I’m trying to remember when it started, and it all gets kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the times when you feel fired up and capable are related to a specific season or time of year?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my work kind of has seasons, so it’s hard to separate it out. Like spring rolls around and suddenly everyone wants mulch, lawns cleaned up, beds edged, all that stuff, and you’re just moving more anyway. So maybe I feel more capable then because there’s more to do? But I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some specific season where it always happens.\n\nI guess I haven’t tracked it like that. Half the time I only realize afterward that I was running on that fired-up feeling, and then I’m like, “Was that because of work, or the weather, or just me?” I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, but… yeah, it seems like I end up down after. Or maybe I’m already kind of down and then that fired-up part just makes it look different for a while.\n\nThe thing is, this depressed feeling has been hanging around for about a year now. Like it’s just been there in the background while I’m mowing lawns or fixing equipment or whatever, and then this latest crash after feeling capable and all that… that’s the worst it’s gotten. So I guess after those times, yeah, it can really drop off hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think I already kind of went into this, but it does feel like I end up depressed after those fired-up stretches. This low feeling’s been hanging over me for about a year, honestly.\n\nAnd this last time, after I had that “I can handle everything” kind of stretch, the crash has been the worst it’s gotten. It’s hard to tell where one thing ends and the other starts, because life and work just keep going, you know? But yeah, the depression’s been there a long time, and lately it’s gotten really bad.\n\nDoctor: Is the depressed feeling that follows your fired-up episodes usually the same length of time, or does it vary?\n\nPatient: It varies, I think… or at least I don’t have a clean pattern for it. This one hasn’t felt like a normal “couple days and then I’m back” kind of thing. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for about a year now, like even when work is busy and I’m out doing jobs, it’s still there.\n\nAnd this latest crash after feeling all fired up has been the worst it’s gotten. I couldn’t really tell you if the other times were the same length, because I don’t write it down or anything, and days kind of blur together when you’re just trying to keep up with work. But yeah… this time it’s been going on a long time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that when you're not in one of these fired-up periods, you're generally a more subdued or less confident person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably. When I’m not in that mode, I’m more like just trying to get through the day and keep the jobs paid up, you know?\n\nBut when I’m in it, it’s different. I really believed I was about to become, like, the guy every town around here would call for sustainable landscaping. Like I had this whole picture in my head of being known for it, everybody needing me for that kind of work… even though, realistically, I’m still just doing small jobs and trying to keep everything from falling behind.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I feel like I already kind of said it, but yeah, it usually seems like I come down afterward. This time especially, it’s not like I just felt lousy for a weekend or something.\n\nIt’s been… hanging over me for about a year now, honestly. And after the most recent time where I felt really fired up and like I could take on everything, the crash after that has been the worst it’s gotten. I still go to work and do what I have to do, mostly, but it’s just been sitting there over me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not in one of these periods where you feel fired up and capable, do you feel completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don’t really keep track of it that way. I’d start with one thing and then think, oh, I should call this guy, and then there’s this other nursery out by where my cousin lives—anyway, it just got to be a lot, and people were looking at me like I was doing too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually happy or excited lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, happy isn’t exactly the word, but kind of. I felt really fired up, like I was bigger than myself or something, like I could make all this stuff happen if people would just get out of the way.\n\nAnd then if one of the guys at work was like, “Maybe slow down,” or questioned what I was doing, I’d just snap. Like way harder than it called for. I’d hear myself afterward and be like, why did I come at him like that? But in the moment it felt like they were messing up this whole thing I had going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable or snapping at people consistently over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Before I kind of crashed, I was really fired up—like, I don’t know, almost bigger than myself, like I had this whole plan and everybody should just see it.\n\nBut yeah, if one of the guys questioned me or said I should slow down, I’d snap. Like, way harder than made sense. Even little stuff felt like they were getting in the way. I remember one coworker just asking if we had approval for something, and I went off on him like he’d insulted me or something. Then later I’m thinking, why did I do that? But at the time it felt totally justified.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty at any point recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… sad or empty? I mean, maybe tired of all of it, but I’m not sure I’d call it empty. Like, sometimes after work I just sit there and the TV’s on but I’m not really watching, you know?\n\nBut then again, I do that anyway sometimes. My dad used to do that after long days too, just sit in his chair with the news on and not say anything, so I don’t know if that means anything. I guess I haven’t really been thinking of it as “sad,” exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're more important or capable than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I did. I mean, it sounds stupid saying it now, but I honestly thought I was about to be the guy every town around here called for sustainable landscaping. Like if anybody needed it done right, they’d come to me.\n\nAnd the funny thing is, I’m still just trying to keep my small jobs paid up, you know? Like I’ve got regular work, bills, equipment stuff, all that. My cousin was saying the other day how expensive mulch has gotten—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, in my head, it felt like I was way past where I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being fired up and capable something that you experience all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes. It’s not like I’m like that all the time.\n\nThat stretch started when I was kind of remaking myself for that park pitch—like I was getting all into it, thinking about how I’d present myself, what I’d say, even stuff like how the truck looked, which is dumb maybe, but it felt important. And then it just kept going for… I don’t know, about two weeks, maybe. Then I kind of crashed after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these periods of feeling fired up and capable happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being the same time every year. Like, I’m trying to think—spring gets busy for landscaping, obviously, so everybody’s kind of running around then, and fall cleanup has its own thing too. \n\nBut whether I get that… fired-up feeling on a calendar schedule? I couldn’t really tell you. I haven’t kept track like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of feeling really down or depressed after one of these episodes where you felt fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… after that kind of high point, I do come down, but honestly it’s not even like it just started then. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for… I don’t know, about a year now.\n\nThis last crash after feeling all fired up, though—that’s been the worst it’s gotten. Like, worse than the usual heaviness. And it’s weird because work stuff keeps going, grass keeps growing, people still want things done, my truck still needs gas, but I’m just… under it. It’s been a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low periods, like the one you're in now, happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, winter can feel worse just because everything’s gray and work slows down, and then I start thinking too much, you know? But I don’t know if that’s a pattern or just… life being life.\n\nI’ve never really marked it down like, “Oh, every January I get low,” or anything. It’s more like I notice I’m already in it, and then afterward I’m trying to remember when it started, and it all gets kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the times when you feel fired up and capable are related to a specific season or time of year?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my work kind of has seasons, so it’s hard to separate it out. Like spring rolls around and suddenly everyone wants mulch, lawns cleaned up, beds edged, all that stuff, and you’re just moving more anyway. So maybe I feel more capable then because there’s more to do? But I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some specific season where it always happens.\n\nI guess I haven’t tracked it like that. Half the time I only realize afterward that I was running on that fired-up feeling, and then I’m like, “Was that because of work, or the weather, or just me?” I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, but… yeah, it seems like I end up down after. Or maybe I’m already kind of down and then that fired-up part just makes it look different for a while.\n\nThe thing is, this depressed feeling has been hanging around for about a year now. Like it’s just been there in the background while I’m mowing lawns or fixing equipment or whatever, and then this latest crash after feeling capable and all that… that’s the worst it’s gotten. So I guess after those times, yeah, it can really drop off hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think I already kind of went into this, but it does feel like I end up depressed after those fired-up stretches. This low feeling’s been hanging over me for about a year, honestly.\n\nAnd this last time, after I had that “I can handle everything” kind of stretch, the crash has been the worst it’s gotten. It’s hard to tell where one thing ends and the other starts, because life and work just keep going, you know? But yeah, the depression’s been there a long time, and lately it’s gotten really bad.\n\nDoctor: Is the depressed feeling that follows your fired-up episodes usually the same length of time, or does it vary?\n\nPatient: It varies, I think… or at least I don’t have a clean pattern for it. This one hasn’t felt like a normal “couple days and then I’m back” kind of thing. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for about a year now, like even when work is busy and I’m out doing jobs, it’s still there.\n\nAnd this latest crash after feeling all fired up has been the worst it’s gotten. I couldn’t really tell you if the other times were the same length, because I don’t write it down or anything, and days kind of blur together when you’re just trying to keep up with work. But yeah… this time it’s been going on a long time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that when you're not in one of these fired-up periods, you're generally a more subdued or less confident person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably. When I’m not in that mode, I’m more like just trying to get through the day and keep the jobs paid up, you know?\n\nBut when I’m in it, it’s different. I really believed I was about to become, like, the guy every town around here would call for sustainable landscaping. Like I had this whole picture in my head of being known for it, everybody needing me for that kind of work… even though, realistically, I’m still just doing small jobs and trying to keep everything from falling behind.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I feel like I already kind of said it, but yeah, it usually seems like I come down afterward. I still go to work and do what I have to do, mostly, but it’s just been sitting there over me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not in one of these periods where you feel fired up and capable, do you feel completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I guess I keep thinking “normal” should mean I’m just back to myself, going to work, doing the yards, dealing with customers, all that. And I do some of that, but it’s like there’s this weight still there.\n\nHonestly, the depression’s been hanging over me for about a year now. It’s not like I come down and then I’m fine again after a couple days. This latest crash has been the worst it’s gotten… like even when I’m technically functioning, I don’t feel back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can recall specific times when you shifted from feeling normal to feeling fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I can think of times. Yeah… I think so. Not like I wake up and go, “okay, this is a period,” you know? But looking back, before I crashed, there were stretches where I felt really fired up, like I was almost bigger than myself or something.

Like I’ve got regular work, bills, equipment stuff, all that. My cousin was saying the other day how expensive mulch has gotten—sorry, that’s not really the point. The point is, in my head, it felt like I was way past where I actually am.\n\nDoctor: Is this feeling of being fired up and capable something that you experience all the time, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes. It’s not like I’m like that all the time.\n\nThat stretch started when I was kind of remaking myself for that park pitch—like I was getting all into it, thinking about how I’d present myself, what I’d say, even stuff like how the truck looked, which is dumb maybe, but it felt important. And then it just kept going for… I don’t know, about two weeks, maybe. Then I kind of crashed after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these periods of feeling fired up and capable happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’ve noticed it being the same time every year. Like, I’m trying to think—spring gets busy for landscaping, obviously, so everybody’s kind of running around then, and fall cleanup has its own thing too. \n\nBut whether I get that… fired-up feeling on a calendar schedule? I couldn’t really tell you. I haven’t kept track like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of feeling really down or depressed after one of these episodes where you felt fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… after that kind of high point, I do come down, but honestly it’s not even like it just started then. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for… I don’t know, about a year now.\n\nThis last crash after feeling all fired up, though—that’s been the worst it’s gotten. Like, worse than the usual heaviness. And it’s weird because work stuff keeps going, grass keeps growing, people still want things done, my truck still needs gas, but I’m just… under it. It’s been a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low periods, like the one you're in now, happen at the same time every year?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, winter can feel worse just because everything’s gray and work slows down, and then I start thinking too much, you know? But I don’t know if that’s a pattern or just… life being life.\n\nI’ve never really marked it down like, “Oh, every January I get low,” or anything. It’s more like I notice I’m already in it, and then afterward I’m trying to remember when it started, and it all gets kind of blurry.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the times when you feel fired up and capable are related to a specific season or time of year?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my work kind of has seasons, so it’s hard to separate it out. Like spring rolls around and suddenly everyone wants mulch, lawns cleaned up, beds edged, all that stuff, and you’re just moving more anyway. So maybe I feel more capable then because there’s more to do? But I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed some specific season where it always happens.\n\nI guess I haven’t tracked it like that. Half the time I only realize afterward that I was running on that fired-up feeling, and then I’m like, “Was that because of work, or the weather, or just me?” I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say always, but… yeah, it seems like I end up down after. Or maybe I’m already kind of down and then that fired-up part just makes it look different for a while.\n\nThe thing is, this depressed feeling has been hanging around for about a year now. Like it’s just been there in the background while I’m mowing lawns or fixing equipment or whatever, and then this latest crash after feeling capable and all that… that’s the worst it’s gotten. So I guess after those times, yeah, it can really drop off hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think I already kind of went into this, but it does feel like I end up depressed after those fired-up stretches. This low feeling’s been hanging over me for about a year, honestly.\n\nAnd this last time, after I had that “I can handle everything” kind of stretch, the crash has been the worst it’s gotten. It’s hard to tell where one thing ends and the other starts, because life and work just keep going, you know? But yeah, the depression’s been there a long time, and lately it’s gotten really bad.\n\nDoctor: Is the depressed feeling that follows your fired-up episodes usually the same length of time, or does it vary?\n\nPatient: It varies, I think… or at least I don’t have a clean pattern for it. This one hasn’t felt like a normal “couple days and then I’m back” kind of thing. The depressed feeling has been hanging over me for about a year now, like even when work is busy and I’m out doing jobs, it’s still there.\n\nAnd this latest crash after feeling all fired up has been the worst it’s gotten. I couldn’t really tell you if the other times were the same length, because I don’t write it down or anything, and days kind of blur together when you’re just trying to keep up with work. But yeah… this time it’s been going on a long time.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that when you're not in one of these fired-up periods, you're generally a more subdued or less confident person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably. When I’m not in that mode, I’m more like just trying to get through the day and keep the jobs paid up, you know?\n\nBut when I’m in it, it’s different. I really believed I was about to become, like, the guy every town around here would call for sustainable landscaping. Like I had this whole picture in my head of being known for it, everybody needing me for that kind of work… even though, realistically, I’m still just doing small jobs and trying to keep everything from falling behind.\n\nDoctor: Do you always feel depressed after one of these episodes where you feel really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I feel like I already kind of said it, but yeah, it usually seems like I come down afterward. This time especially, it’s not like I just felt lousy for a weekend or something.\n\nIt’s been… hanging over me for about a year now, honestly. And after the most recent time where I felt really fired up and like I could take on everything, the crash after that has been the worst it’s gotten. I still go to work and do what I have to do, mostly, but it’s just been sitting there over me.\n\nDoctor: When you're not in one of these periods where you feel fired up and capable, do you feel completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I guess I keep thinking “normal” should mean I’m just back to myself, going to work, doing the yards, dealing with customers, all that. And I do some of that, but it’s like there’s this weight still there.\n\nHonestly, the depression’s been hanging over me for about a year now. It’s not like I come down and then I’m fine again after a couple days. This latest crash has been the worst it’s gotten… like even when I’m technically functioning, I don’t feel back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can recall specific times when you shifted from feeling normal to feeling fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I can think of times. It’s not always like a light switch, but before I crashed I remember feeling really fired up, like… almost bigger than myself, I guess. Like I could handle way more than I normally would.\n\nAnd it would show up at work too. If one of the guys questioned my plan or told me I should slow down, I’d snap at him way harder than made sense. Like, over something small, and then later I’d think, why did I come at him like that? But in the moment it felt like they were getting in the way, you know?\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling fired up and capable, do you feel like you're able to get by on less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t really keep track of it that way. My sleep’s kind of tied up with work sometimes, like if we’ve got an early job or weather coming in, I’ll be up thinking about routes or equipment or whatever… but I don’t know if that’s the same as needing less sleep.\n\nSo… I guess I can’t really say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually need less sleep when you're feeling really fired up and capable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “usually,” no. I mean, I might be up later because my head’s going or I’m thinking about the next day, but that also happens when work’s busy, like when we’ve got a big property to get through or the mower’s acting up and I’m trying to figure out how to make the schedule work.\n\nSo… I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I need less sleep. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling fired up and capable, do you feel like you're in a distinct episode or period that's separate from your normal state?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like I wake up and go, “okay, this is a period,” you know? But looking back, before I crashed, there were stretches where I felt really fired up, like I was almost bigger than myself or something.\n\nAnd it was different from normal because at work I’d get this thing where if one of the guys questioned my plan or told me to slow down, I’d snap at him way harder than made sense. Like I remember once—well, it happened more than once—but it’d be some small thing about how we were loading gear or doing a route, and I’d act like they were challenging me personally. Then later I’d be like, why did I react like that? So yeah, I guess it did feel separate when I think back on it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
